The assertion gate removes 18 of them, costs two gold triples, and buys 0.008 of precision, because the <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">status</code> question has nothing to say about a plainly stated non-relation. The selector&#x27;s own probability is the right filter, and because it&#x27;s a threshold on stored numbers, the whole frontier comes from one pass:</p>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi"><img src="/static/images/typesafe-jev-knowledge-graph-extraction/selection-frontier.svg" alt="Relation selection at paragraph scope, one pass and every threshold. Selection alone sits below GLiNER; the union with GLiNER moves recall to the right." class="css-pujt2y"/></p>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi">Keep only the selections at probability 0.95 or above, gate them, and union them with GLiNER&#x27;s gated edges:</p>
<div tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Table" class="css-104nsfo"><table class="css-1yma1ta"><thead><tr><th class="css-19x6mbd">System</th><th class="css-19x6mbd">pred / match</th><th class="css-19x6mbd">precision</th><th class="css-19x6mbd">recall</th><th class="css-19x6mbd">F1</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="css-v9ldn8">GLiNER plus gate (notebook 10&#x27;s best)</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">47 / 19</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.404</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.404</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.404</td></tr><tr><td class="css-v9ldn8">plus paragraph-scope selection at 0.95, gated</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">58 / 23</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.397</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.489</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.438</td></tr><tr><td class="css-v9ldn8">plus document-scope selection at 0.95, gated</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">66 / 25</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.379</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.532</td><td class="css-v9ldn8"><strong>0.442</strong></td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi"><strong>F1 0.442 is the best any configuration in the repo has produced on this corpus</strong>, and document-scope selection on its own reaches recall 0.596 - 28 of 47 gold triples, the most any system has recovered - at precision 0.169. Document scope costs three times the requests for four thousandths of F1 and four points of recall; whether that&#x27;s worth it depends on what the graph is for.</p>
<h2 class="chakra-heading css-qxamfn" id="three-more-experiments"><div class="css-12m0k8p">Three More Experiments<a href="#three-more-experiments" class="anchor-link css-fyjql5" aria-hidden="true" tabindex="-1">#</a></div></h2>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi">Notebooks 12 through 14 take the same model to three other stages, and each reduces to one lesson.</p>
<h3 class="chakra-heading css-169o8ex" id="what-contradicts-what"><div class="css-12m0k8p">What Contradicts What<a href="#what-contradicts-what" class="anchor-link css-fyjql5" aria-hidden="true" tabindex="-1">#</a></div></h3>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi">The repo&#x27;s agent-memory layer supersedes <em>&quot;I use npm&quot;</em> with <em>&quot;I&#x27;ve switched to pnpm&quot;</em> but keeps both <em>&quot;I use Python&quot;</em> and <em>&quot;I&#x27;m based in Berlin&quot;</em>, which means deciding whether two values are <em>alternatives</em>, and whether <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">npm</code> and <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">pnpm</code> compete for the same role is not a thing a string, an embedding, or a span model knows. One <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Noul</code> - <em>are <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">a</code> and <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">b</code> alternatives, two options filling the same role?</em> - is the whole integration:</p>
<pre tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Code block" class="language-python css-0"><code class="language-python">memory <span class="token operator">=</span> TemporalGraph<span class="token punctuation">(</span>alternative_fn<span class="token operator">=</span>JevAlternatives<span class="token punctuation">(</span>ts<span class="token punctuation">)</span><span class="token punctuation">)</span>
</code></pre>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi">Asked over every pair of the things the gold memory facts name, it puts the two planted switches at 0.92 (<code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">npm</code>/<code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">pnpm</code>) and 0.63 (<code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Python</code>/<code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Go</code>) and all 26 other pairs at 0.21 or below. MiniLM cosine puts <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Python</code>/<code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Go</code> at 0.166, lower than 20 of the 26 non-alternatives, because it measures how alike two names are, and <em>being alternatives</em> is not that:</p>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi"><img src="/static/images/typesafe-jev-knowledge-graph-extraction/alternatives-noul-vs-cosine.png" alt="One Noul separates the two planted alternatives from all 26 other pairs with a single threshold. Cosine similarity cannot, because Go and Python do not look alike - they compete for the same job." class="css-pujt2y"/></p>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi">In the actual ingest loop it never got to show it: extraction delivered exactly one real switch, and <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">prefers pnpm</code> had been extracted from the session in which the user <em>turned pnpm down</em>, a modality trap of the kind the assertion gate catches, one stage upstream. <strong>The judge is only as good as its queue.</strong> That&#x27;s the third stage in a row to say so.</p>
<h3 class="chakra-heading css-169o8ex" id="a-bigger-model-is-not-a-curator"><div class="css-12m0k8p">A Bigger Model Is Not A Curator<a href="#a-bigger-model-is-not-a-curator" class="anchor-link css-fyjql5" aria-hidden="true" tabindex="-1">#</a></div></h3>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi"><a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai/patterns/confidence-routing">Confidence-gated routing</a> says: let the fast model decide everything it&#x27;s sure about and send the rest to something more expensive. Notebook 13 sends the 31 of 170 edge judgments the System One model was unsure about to Claude Haiku. Haiku agreed on 28 of them; the three it flipped were two coin-flip copies of the headline acquisition and one spurious <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">impacts</code> edge that Haiku asserted. That last one is the whole result:</p>
<div tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Table" class="css-104nsfo"><table class="css-1yma1ta"><thead><tr><th class="css-19x6mbd">System</th><th class="css-19x6mbd">precision</th><th class="css-19x6mbd">recall</th><th class="css-19x6mbd">F1</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="css-v9ldn8">System One gate alone</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.404</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.404</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.404</td></tr><tr><td class="css-v9ldn8">cascade: uncertain edges to Haiku</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.396</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.404</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.400</td></tr><tr><td class="css-v9ldn8">Haiku on every edge</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.373</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.404</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">0.388</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi">Recall never moves; precision falls as escalation rises. Both judges&#x27; answers are on disk, so the band can be swept from <em>escalate nothing</em> to <em>escalate everything</em> at no cost, and the sweep is flat, then down:</p>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi"><img src="/static/images/typesafe-jev-knowledge-graph-extraction/cascade-sweep.svg" alt="Confidence-gated escalation swept from 0% to 100% of edges: flat, then down. There is no width of the band at which the slow judge improves on the fast one." class="css-pujt2y"/></p>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi">The best escalation rate on this corpus is zero, for about twelve cents of Haiku at 3.6 seconds a call: low confidence meant <em>the text is genuinely ambiguous</em>, and a bigger model doesn&#x27;t resolve ambiguity that&#x27;s in the source. The resolution side is sharper. Fourteen pairs landed on the adjudicator&#x27;s middle level, the curator queue, and six of them are gold-same: <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Northwind</code> against <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Northwind Logistics Inc.</code>, deliberately left undecided at levels 0.87 to 1.24. Haiku called two of them <em>different</em> (<em>&quot;the entities have different legal names&quot;</em>) and four <em>unsure</em>. <strong>Zero of six.</strong> It reasoned its way to the wrong answer fluently, at twenty times the latency, where a calibrated abstention was the better answer. The review level exists so that a <em>person</em> with a reference to hand can look, and a larger language model is not that person.</p>
<h3 class="chakra-heading css-169o8ex" id="the-boundary-of-the-no-llm-position"><div class="css-12m0k8p">The Boundary Of The No-LLM Position<a href="#the-boundary-of-the-no-llm-position" class="anchor-link css-fyjql5" aria-hidden="true" tabindex="-1">#</a></div></h3>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi">GLiNER classifies the intent of the repo&#x27;s eight customer-support threads near chance and their priority <em>at</em> chance, because intent is written down and severity is not. Notebook 14 sends the threads to Jev instead: a <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Choice</code> over six intents, a <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Score</code> over four priorities written as operational situations, and a <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Noul</code> for whether the thread ended resolved.</p>
<div tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Table" class="css-104nsfo"><table class="css-1yma1ta"><thead><tr><th class="css-19x6mbd"></th><th class="css-19x6mbd">GLiNER2.5</th><th class="css-19x6mbd">System One</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="css-v9ldn8">intent</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">3 of 8</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">5 of 8</td></tr><tr><td class="css-v9ldn8">priority</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">2 of 8</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">3 of 8</td></tr><tr><td class="css-v9ldn8">resolved</td><td class="css-v9ldn8">not attempted</td><td class="css-v9ldn8"><strong>8 of 8</strong></td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi"><strong>What is written down, the model reads.</strong> Whether a thread ended resolved is in the customer&#x27;s last turn, and the <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Noul</code> puts the two open threads at 0.05 and 0.08 and the six resolved ones between 0.64 and 0.97. Priority stays at chance, and the misses are <em>confident</em>: thread eight&#x27;s customer says <em>&quot;Small one&quot;</em>, the gold says <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">low</code>, and the model says <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">urgent</code> at 2.71, on a scale of my own writing that the gold labeller never saw. Routing on confidence sent the three hedged calls to a person and let every confident disagreement through. <strong>Confidence routes model uncertainty. It cannot route definitional disagreement.</strong> Fix the question, not the threshold.</p>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi">The judgments went on a <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Thread</code> node as properties, and the query that motivated all of it is one a span model could never have answered:</p>
<pre tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Code block" class="language-cypher css-0"><code class="language-cypher"><span class="token comment">// open threads that need a person, most urgent first</span>
<span class="token keyword">MATCH</span> <span class="token punctuation">(</span>c<span class="token operator">:</span><span class="token class-name">Customer</span><span class="token punctuation">)</span><span class="token operator">-</span><span class="token punctuation">[</span><span class="token operator">:</span><span class="token relationship property">OPENED</span><span class="token punctuation">]</span><span class="token operator">-&gt;</span><span class="token punctuation">(</span>t<span class="token operator">:</span><span class="token class-name">Thread</span><span class="token punctuation">)</span>
<span class="token keyword">WHERE</span> t<span class="token punctuation">.</span>resolved <span class="token operator">=</span> <span class="token boolean">false</span>
  <span class="token keyword">AND</span> <span class="token punctuation">(</span>t<span class="token punctuation">.</span>priority <span class="token keyword">IN</span> <span class="token punctuation">[</span><span class="token string">&#x27;high&#x27;</span><span class="token punctuation">,</span> <span class="token string">&#x27;urgent&#x27;</span><span class="token punctuation">]</span> <span class="token keyword">OR</span> t<span class="token punctuation">.</span>route <span class="token operator">=</span> <span class="token string">&#x27;review&#x27;</span><span class="token punctuation">)</span>
<span class="token keyword">RETURN</span> c<span class="token punctuation">.</span>name<span class="token punctuation">,</span> t<span class="token punctuation">.</span>thread_id<span class="token punctuation">,</span> t<span class="token punctuation">.</span>intent<span class="token punctuation">,</span> t<span class="token punctuation">.</span>priority<span class="token punctuation">,</span> t<span class="token punctuation">.</span>priority_score<span class="token punctuation">,</span> t<span class="token punctuation">.</span>route
<span class="token keyword">ORDER</span> <span class="token keyword">BY</span> t<span class="token punctuation">.</span>priority_score <span class="token keyword">DESC</span>
</code></pre>
<h2 class="chakra-heading css-qxamfn" id="what-i-took-away"><div class="css-12m0k8p">What I Took Away<a href="#what-i-took-away" class="anchor-link css-fyjql5" aria-hidden="true" tabindex="-1">#</a></div></h2>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi"><strong>Candidate generation and judgment are different jobs.</strong> The assertion gate worked because GLiNER had already produced the candidates; the adjudicator scored perfectly and bought nothing until something produced the candidates it needed. Extract locally and exhaustively, judge selectively, keep the judgments as data.</p>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi"><strong>Read the distribution, not the label.</strong> A <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Score</code> of 1.41 at confidence 0.12 is two readings, not one, and a type <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Choice</code> at 0.56 versus 0.41 is a request to be allowed both. Seventeen clones took an unchanged resolver to perfect recall.</p>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi"><strong>Ambiguity in the question surfaces as a confident answer to the wrong reading, not as an uncertain answer.</strong> Keep the distinction in the answer type, or state it in the question, and validate the wording against controls.</p>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi"><strong>Store the raw judgments.</strong> The gate becomes a <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">WHERE</code> clause, the reason for every drop survives, and when a merge looks wrong six months from now the diagnostic <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Noul</code>s are there to read.</p>
<p class="chakra-text css-1x3gqzi">Jev is not a replacement for either of the other extractors. GLiNER2.5 produced 170 candidate edges in fourteen seconds on a CPU with no key, and a System One model cannot do that. What it did was answer several hundred questions about those candidates that neither a span model nor a threshold could, in typed form, in under a fifth of a second per request.</p>
<h2 class="chakra-heading css-qxamfn" id="resources"><div class="css-12m0k8p">Resources<a href="#resources" class="anchor-link css-fyjql5" aria-hidden="true" tabindex="-1">#</a></div></h2>
<ul class="css-6vq53b">
<li class="css-bxak8j"><a href="https://github.com/johnymontana/extraction-knowledge-graph-experiments">extraction-knowledge-graph-experiments on GitHub</a> - the repo: notebooks 01 through 14, the <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">kgx</code> library, and the synthetic corpora with gold labels</li>
<li class="css-bxak8j">The five notebooks behind this post: <a href="https://github.com/johnymontana/extraction-knowledge-graph-experiments/blob/master/notebooks/10_typesafe_system_one.ipynb">typed judgments</a>, <a href="https://github.com/johnymontana/extraction-knowledge-graph-experiments/blob/master/notebooks/11_typesafe_relation_selection.ipynb">selection, not generation</a>, <a href="https://github.com/johnymontana/extraction-knowledge-graph-experiments/blob/master/notebooks/12_typesafe_contradictions.ipynb">what contradicts what</a>, <a href="https://github.com/johnymontana/extraction-knowledge-graph-experiments/blob/master/notebooks/13_typesafe_cascade.ipynb">escalate on confidence</a>, <a href="https://github.com/johnymontana/extraction-knowledge-graph-experiments/blob/master/notebooks/14_typesafe_document_judgments.ipynb">the boundary of the no-LLM position</a></li>
<li class="css-bxak8j"><a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai">TypeSafe documentation</a> - <a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai/concepts/system-one">System One</a>, the three <a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai/primitives">primitives</a>, <a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai/confidence">confidence</a>, and the <a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai/sdk/python">Python SDK</a></li>
<li class="css-bxak8j"><a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai/cookbooks/entity_alignment">Knowledge graph entity alignment cookbook</a> - the <code class="chakra-code css-1nzxbu7" style="background-color:#f1f5f9;color:#1e293b;border:1px solid #e2e8f0">Score</code>-with-three-levels shape used for pair adjudication</li>
<li class="css-bxak8j"><a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai/patterns/confidence-routing">Confidence-gated routing</a> and <a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai/patterns/composite-scoring">composite scoring</a> - the two TypeSafe patterns this post leans on</li>
<li class="css-bxak8j"><a href="https://fastino.ai/models/gliner2-5">GLiNER2.5</a> - the span extractor that produces every candidate in these experiments</li>
</ul>
